\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{(0,\infty)^{d-1}}\rho(a,{\bm{b}})\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}(b_{j})_{1\leq j\leq d,j\neq i}\right)\mathrm{d}b_{i}.

Now, it is well known that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=P\left(\exists\ C\in(0,\infty)\text{ such that }\tilde{N}^{(i)}\left(\{C\}\right)>1\right)

\displaystyle=P\left(\exists\ C\in(0,\infty)\text{ such that }\tilde{N}\left((0,\infty)\times\{{\bm{b}}\in(0,\infty)^{d}\mid b_{i}=C\}\right)>1\right)

since
\tilde{N}^{(i)}
is a simple point process on
(0,\infty)
as it has a diffuse intensity measure, see
[
23
, Lemma 3.6]
. Thus, each atom of
N=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}a_{k}\delta_{{\bm{b}}_{k}}
has components
(b_{k,i})_{{1\leq i\leq d}}
that are almost surely distinct from all components of the atoms
(b_{\underline{k},i})_{{1\leq i\leq d}}
whenever
k\neq\underline{k}
.

The claim that
\beta_{\theta}
is supported on
G^{\Psi(\theta)}
now immediately follows as the atoms
(a_{k},{\bm{b}}_{k})
corresponding to the exchangeable sequences
{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(k)}
in (
42
) correspond to almost surely district atoms with distinct components.

To prove the singularity, assume that
\beta_{\theta}(G^{\Psi})>0
for some collection of partitions
\Psi\neq\Psi(\theta)
. Then, there exist
i
,
j\neq\underline{j}
such that either
(i,j),(i,\underline{j})\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}(\theta)
with
\beta_{\theta}\left(y_{i,j}\neq y_{i,\underline{j}}\right)>0
or
(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}(\theta)
and
(i,\underline{j})\in\Psi^{(i)}_{\underline{k}}(\theta)
for some
k\neq\underline{k}
with
\beta_{\theta}\left(y_{i,j}=y_{i,\underline{j}}\right)>0
. In the first case there would exists a sequence
{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(k)}
in (
42
) such that
{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(k)}
is supported on at least two distinct finite atoms, which has probability
0
. In the second case there would exist
{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(k)}
and
{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(\underline{k})}
for which
b_{k,i}=b_{\underline{k},i}
for
k\neq\underline{k}
, which has probability
0
. Therefore, singularity of
\beta_{\theta}
and
\beta_{\tilde{\theta}}
follows whenever
\Psi(\theta)\neq\Psi(\tilde{\theta})
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
12.4

Proof.

The general idea for the proof is to plug-in Proposition
12.2
into (
30
) to verify that
\beta_{\theta}
has the claimed density. First, note that
\beta_{\theta}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{IJ})
is concentrated in
\mathbb{R}^{|IJ|}
and thus we only need to consider
{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\theta_{l}|}
when applying Proposition
12.2
, which ensures that
I({\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}})=I(\theta_{l})
. Recall that the mass of
\beta_{\theta}
is determined via its mass on
G^{\Psi(\theta)}
, which is a
d_{\Psi(\theta)}
-dimensional subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{|IJ|}
. Then, for
\left(c_{i,k}\right)_{1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\Psi}}
and recalling the measures
\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a\delta_{{\bm{b}}}\right)\right)^{(IJ)}
from (
50
), (
30
) implies that
\beta_{\theta}
is uniquely determined by expressions of the form

\displaystyle\beta_{\theta}\left(\big\{{\bm{x}}_{IJ}\in G^{\Psi(\theta)}\mid x_{i,j}\leq c_{i,k}\ \forall(i,j)\in\Psi_{k}^{(i)},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\big\}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\times_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\int_{(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)^{d}}\int_{(0,\infty)^{|\theta_{l}|}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\{x_{i,j}\leq c_{i,k}\ \forall(i,j)\in\Psi_{k}^{(i)},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\exp\left(-\lambda^{(IJ)}\left(({\bm{x}_{IJ}},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\left(\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}K_{\theta_{l}}\left({\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}},\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}>{\bm{x}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\otimes_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}}\right)\right)^{(\theta_{l})}\left(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\right)\rho(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\mathrm{d}(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\times_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\int_{(0,\infty)\times\{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}\mid b_{i}^{(l)}\leq c_{i,k}\ \forall\ (i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\times_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\int_{\{{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\mid x_{i,j}=b^{(l)}_{i}\ \forall(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\exp\left(-\lambda^{(IJ)}\left(({\bm{x}_{IJ}},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\left(\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}K_{\theta_{l}}\left({\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}},\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}>{\bm{x}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\otimes_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}}\right)\right)^{(\theta_{l})}\left(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\right)\right)\left(\otimes_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\rho(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\mathrm{d}(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\right)=:\#_{1}

where we have used that
{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\theta_{l}|}
and thus its
i
-margins consist of the corresponding values from
{\bm{b}}^{(l)}
, which enforces that
b^{(l)}_{i}\leq c_{i,k}
for all
i,k
s.t. there exists a
j
with
(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l}
. We further exploit this tie structure to plug-in the corresponding values of
{\bm{b}}^{(l)}
into
{\bm{x}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}
and
{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}
to obtain

\displaystyle\#_{1}=\left(\times_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\int_{(0,\infty)\times\{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}\mid b_{i}^{(l)}\leq c_{i,k}\ \forall\ (i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\times_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\int_{\{{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\mid x_{i,j}=b^{(l)}_{i}\ \forall(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\exp\left(-\lambda^{(IJ)}\left((\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\left(\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}K_{\theta_{l}}\left(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}>\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\otimes_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}}\right)\right)^{(\theta_{l})}\right)\left(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\right)\left(\otimes_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\rho(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\mathrm{d}(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\right)=:\#_{2}

where
\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}}=\left(b^{(l)}_{i}\right)_{(i,j)\in\theta_{l}}
,
\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{L(\theta)}b_{i}^{(k)}\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)\in\theta_{k}\}}\right)_{(i,j)\in IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}
and

\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ}:=

\left(\sum_{k=1}^{L(\theta)}b_{i}^{(k)}\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)\in\theta_{k}\}}\right)_{(i,j)\in IJ}
. Noticing that the integrand only depends on

\left(\left(b_{i}^{(l)}\right)_{i\in I(\theta_{l})}\right)_{1\leq l\leq L(\theta)}

we may rearrange the terms to integrate out the remaining
\left((b_{i}^{(l)})_{i\in I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}}\right)_{1\leq l\leq L(\theta)}
to obtain

\displaystyle\#_{2}=\left(\times_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\int_{\{(b_{i}^{(l)})_{i\in I(\theta_{l})}\mid b_{i}^{(l)}\leq c_{i,k}\ \forall\ (i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\exp\left(-\lambda^{(IJ)}\left((\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\Bigg(K_{\theta_{l}}\left(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}>\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\Bigg(\int_{(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)^{|I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}|}}\int_{\{{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\mid x_{i,j}=b^{(l)}_{i}\ \forall(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}}\right)\right)^{(\theta_{l})}\left(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\right)\rho(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\mathrm{d}\left(a^{(l)},(b_{i}^{(l)})_{i\in I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}}\right)\Bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\otimes_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\mathrm{d}((b_{i}^{(l)})_{i\in I(\theta_{l})})\right)=:\#_{3}

The key step now is to observe that the denominator
C(\tilde{{\bm{b}}}_{\theta_{l}})
of
K_{\theta_{l}}\left(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}>\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\right)
is equal to

\displaystyle\int_{(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)^{|I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}|}}\int_{\{{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\mid x_{i,j}=b^{(l)}_{i}\ \forall(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}

\displaystyle\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}}\right)\right)^{(\theta_{l})}\left(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\right)\rho(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\mathrm{d}\left(a^{(l)},\left(b_{i}^{(l)}\right)_{i\in I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}}\right),

since, in the notation of Proposition
12.2
,
I(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}})=I(\theta_{l})
and
(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})(I(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}}))={\bm{b}}^{(l)}
and

\displaystyle\int_{\{{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\mid x_{i,j}=b^{(l)}_{i}\ \forall(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),{1\leq i\leq d}\}}\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}}\right)\right)^{(\theta_{l})}\left(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{{\bm{b}}^{(l)}}\right)\right)\left(\{{\underline{\bm{x}}}\mid{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}={\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}}\}\right),

which leads to the cancellation of
C(\tilde{{\bm{b}}}_{\theta_{l}})
in
\#_{3}
. Thus, only the enumerator of

K_{\theta_{l}}\left(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}>\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\right)
remains and we have

\displaystyle\#_{3}=\left(\times_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\int_{\{(b_{i}^{(l)})_{i\in I(\theta_{l})}\mid b_{i}^{(l)}\leq c_{i,k}\ \forall\ (i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\subset\theta_{l},1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}),i\in I(IJ)\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\exp\left(-\lambda^{(IJ)}\left((\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\Bigg(\int_{(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)^{|I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}|}}\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})(I(\theta_{l}))}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\mid{\bm{y}}_{\theta_{l}}=\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}>\tilde{\bm{b}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\rho(a^{(l)},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})\mathrm{d}\left(a^{(l)},(b_{i}^{(l)})_{i\in I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}}\right)\Bigg)\left(\otimes_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\mathrm{d}((b_{i}^{(l)})_{i\in I(\theta_{l})})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\times_{i=1}^{d}\times_{k=1}^{L(\Psi^{(i)}(\theta))}\int_{-\infty}^{c_{i,k}}\right)q_{\theta}\left({\bm{b}}_{IJ}\right)\left(\otimes_{i=1}^{d}\otimes_{k=1}^{L(\Psi^{(i)}(\theta))}\mathrm{d}b_{i}^{(k)}\right)

where we defined

\displaystyle q_{\theta}({\bm{b}}_{IJ}):=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\lambda^{(IJ)}\left(({\bm{b}}_{IJ},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\Bigg(\int_{(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)^{|I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}|}}\left(\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a^{(l)}\delta_{(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})(I(\theta_{l}))}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\Big(\{{\underline{\bm{x}}}\mid{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}={\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},{\bm{x}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}>{\bm{b}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\Big)\rho\left(a^{(l)},(\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}},{\bm{b}}^{(l)})(I(\theta_{l}))\right)\mathrm{d}\left(a^{(l)},(b_{i}^{(l)})_{i\in I(\theta_{l})^{\complement}}\right)\Bigg)

and
{\bm{b}}_{IJ}:=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{L(\Psi^{(i)}(\theta))}b_{i}^{(k)}\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\}}\right)_{(i,j)\in IJ}
,
{\bm{b}}_{\theta_{l}}:=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{L(\Psi^{(i)}(\theta))}b_{i}^{(k)}\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\}}\right)_{(i,j)\in\theta_{l}}
and
{\bm{b}}_{IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}:=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{L(\Psi^{(i)}(\theta))}b_{i}^{(k)}\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)\in\Psi^{(i)}_{k}\}}\right)_{(i,j)\in IJ\setminus\theta_{l}}
are unique representations of the arguments of
q_{\theta}
which only depend
\Psi(\theta)
, implicitly using
\sum_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}|I(\theta_{l})|=\sum_{i\in I(IJ)}L(\Psi^{(i)})
.
Therefore,
\beta_{\theta}
has density
q_{\theta}
which has the claimed representation as a
d_{\Psi}
-dimensional density w.r.t. Lebesgue measure, which proves the claim.

∎

Proof of Corollary
12.5

Proof.

Simply note that for any measurable
A\subset G^{\Psi(\theta)}
we have

\displaystyle\beta_{\theta}(A)

\displaystyle=\int_{A}q_{\theta}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})\mathrm{d}\left(\left(b_{i}^{(k)}\right)_{1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}(\theta))}\right)_{i\in I(IJ)}

\displaystyle=\int_{A}\frac{q_{\theta}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}{\sum_{\overset{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathcal{P}_{IJ}}{\Psi(\theta)=\Psi(\tilde{\theta})}}q_{\tilde{\theta}}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}\Bigg(\sum_{\overset{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathcal{P}_{IJ}}{\Psi(\theta)=\Psi(\tilde{\theta})}}q_{\tilde{\theta}}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\left(\left(b_{i}^{(k)}\right)_{1\leq k\leq L(\Psi^{(i)}(\theta))}\right)_{i\in I(IJ)}

\displaystyle=\int_{A}\frac{q_{\theta}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}{\sum_{\overset{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathcal{P}_{IJ}}{\Psi(\theta)=\Psi(\tilde{\theta})}}q_{\tilde{\theta}}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}\left(\sum_{\overset{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathcal{P}_{IJ}}{\Psi(\theta)=\Psi(\tilde{\theta})}}\beta_{\tilde{\theta}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{b}}_{IJ})\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{A}\frac{q_{\theta}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}{\sum_{\overset{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathcal{P}_{IJ}}{\Psi(\tilde{\theta})}=\Psi({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}q_{\tilde{\theta}}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}\minid\left(\lambda^{(IJ)}\right)\left(\mathrm{d}{\bm{b}}_{IJ}\right),

since
\beta_{\tilde{\theta}}(A)=0
for all
\tilde{\theta}
with
\Psi(\tilde{\theta})\neq\Psi(\theta)
by Lemma
12.3
. Therefore

\frac{\mathrm{d}\beta_{\theta}}{\mathrm{d}\minid\left(\lambda^{(IJ)}\right)}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})=\frac{q_{\theta}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}{\sum_{\overset{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathcal{P}_{IJ}}{\Psi(\theta)=\Psi(\tilde{\theta})}}q_{\tilde{\theta}}({\bm{b}}_{IJ})}.

Moreover, when
\Psi({\bm{X}_{IJ}})\neq\Psi(\theta)
we have
{\bm{X}_{IJ}}\not\in G^{\Psi(\theta)}

\minid\left(\lambda^{(IJ)}\right)
-almost-surely and therefore

\frac{\mathrm{d}\beta_{\theta}}{\mathrm{d}\minid\left(\lambda^{(IJ)}\right)}({\bm{X}_{IJ}})=0\ \minid\left(\lambda^{(IJ)}\right)\text{-almost-surely for all }{\bm{X}_{IJ}}\not\in G^{\Psi(\theta)}.

Thus,

\frac{\mathrm{d}\beta_{\theta}}{\mathrm{d}\minid\left(\lambda^{(IJ)}\right)}({\bm{X}_{IJ}})=\frac{\mathrm{d}\beta_{\theta}}{\mathrm{d}\minid\left(\lambda^{(IJ)}\right)}({\bm{X}_{IJ}})\mathbf{1}_{\{\Psi(\theta)=\Psi({\bm{X}_{IJ}})\}}

\minid\left(\lambda^{(IJ)}\right)
-almost-surely, which proves the claim.
∎

Proof of Theorem
12.1

Proof.

Let
n
such that
IJ\subset d\times n
. According to Theorem
4.4
the posterior Lévy measure
\bar{\nu}_{IJ}
of
\bar{\mu}_{IJ}
is given by

\displaystyle\otimes_{j=1}^{n}\minid(\eta)\left(\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n}\mid{\bm{y}}_{IJ}>{\bm{X}}_{IJ}\}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle=\otimes_{j=1}^{n}\minid(\eta)\left(\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n}\mid{\bm{y}}_{IJ}>{\bm{X}}_{IJ}\}\right)\rho(a,{\bm{b}})\mathrm{d}(a,{\bm{b}})

\displaystyle=\rho(a,{\bm{b}})\exp\left(-a\sum_{j\in J(IJ)}\mathbf{1}_{\{b_{i}\leq X_{i,j}\text{ for some }(i,j)\in IJ\}}\right)\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}{\bm{b}}

which is the Lévy measure of a CRM with the claimed intensity. The distribution of the conditional hitting scenario is given in Corollary
12.5
.

Next, conditionally on the conditional hitting scenario
\tilde{\Theta}
, the distribution of
{\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(n,l)}
from (
18
) is given by

\displaystyle P({\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(n,l)}\in A)=\frac{K_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\left({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},\Big\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\mid\left({\bm{y}}_{n+j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in A;\ {\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\bm{X}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\Big\}\right)}{K_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\left({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\bm{X}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}\right)}

which by Proposition
12.2
is equal to

\displaystyle C(\tilde{\Theta}_{l},{\bm{X}_{IJ}})^{-1}\left(\times_{i=1}^{|I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{\complement}|}\int_{0}^{\infty}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a\delta_{({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\Big\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\mid\left({\bm{y}}_{n+j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in A;\ {\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\bm{X}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}};\ {\bm{y}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}={\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\Big\}\right)

\displaystyle\rho(a,({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}\left((b_{i})_{i\in I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))^{\complement}}\right)

\displaystyle=C(\tilde{\Theta}_{l},{\bm{X}_{IJ}})^{-1}\left(\times_{i=1}^{|I({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}})^{\complement}|}\int_{0}^{\infty}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a\delta_{({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\Big\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\mid\left({\bm{y}}_{n+j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in A\Big\}\right)\otimes_{j=1}^{n}\left(\minid(a\delta_{{\bm{b}}})\right)\left(\Big\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\bm{X}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}};\ {\bm{y}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}={\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\Big\}\right)

\displaystyle\rho(a,({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}\left((b_{i})_{i\in I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))^{\complement}}\right)=:\#_{1}

where the normalizing constant is given by

\displaystyle C(\tilde{\Theta}_{l},{\bm{X}_{IJ}})

\displaystyle:=\left(\times_{i=1}^{|I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{\complement}|}\int_{0}^{\infty}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}\otimes_{j=1}^{n}\left(\minid\left(a\delta_{({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left(\Big\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\bm{X}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}};\ {\bm{y}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}={\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\Big\}\right)\rho(a,({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})))\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}\left((b_{i})_{i\in I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{\complement}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\times_{i=1}^{|I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{\complement}|}\int_{0}^{\infty}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{n}^{(l)}\left(a,\left(b_{i}\right)_{i\in I({\bm{X}}_{\theta_{l}})^{\complement}},{\bm{X}}_{IJ}\right)\rho(a,({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})))\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}\left((b_{i})_{i\in I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{\complement}}\right)

with

\displaystyle\rho_{n}^{(l)}\left(a,\left(b_{i}\right)_{i\in I({\bm{X}}_{\theta_{l}})^{\complement}},{\bm{X}}_{IJ}\right)

\displaystyle:=\otimes_{j=1}^{n}\left(\minid\left(a\delta_{({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))}\right)\right)\left(\big\{{\bm{y}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\bm{X}}_{IJ\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}};\ {\bm{y}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}={\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\big\}\right).

This allows to rewrite

\displaystyle\#_{1}=

\displaystyle C(\tilde{\Theta}_{l},{\bm{X}_{IJ}})^{-1}\left(\times_{i=1}^{|I({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}})^{\complement}|}\int_{0}^{\infty}\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid\left(a\delta_{({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}))}\right)\left(A\right)

\displaystyle\rho_{n}^{(l)}\left(a,\left(b_{i}\right)_{i\in I({\bm{X}}_{\theta_{l}})^{\complement}},{\bm{X}_{IJ}}\right)

\displaystyle\rho(a,({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}\left((b_{i})_{i\in I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))^{\complement}}\right).

Thus,
{\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(n,l)}
can be generated as follows:

1.

Draw
\left(A,\left((B_{i})_{i\in I({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l})})^{\complement}}\right)\right)
according to the density

C(\tilde{\Theta}_{l},{\bm{X}_{IJ}})^{-1}\rho_{n}^{(l)}\left(a,\left(b_{i}\right)_{i\in I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{\complement}},{\bm{X}}_{IJ}\right)\rho(a,({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}\left((b_{i})_{i\in I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))^{\complement}}\right).

.

2.

Conditionally on
\left(A,\left((B_{i})_{i\in I({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l})})^{\complement}}\right)\right)
, define the random measures
\bar{\mu}_{IJ}^{(l)}:=a\delta_{({\bm{X}}_{\Theta_{l}},{\bm{b}})(I(\tilde{\Theta}_{l}))}
and draw an i.i.d. sequence
{\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(n,l)}
with
{\bm{Z}}^{(n,l)}_{j}\sim\minid\left(\bar{\mu}_{IJ}^{(l)}\right)
.

Therefore, the posterior of
\mu
is given by

\mu\mid{\bm{X}_{IJ}}\sim\bar{\mu}_{IJ}+\sum_{l=1}^{L(\tilde{\Theta})}\bar{\mu}_{IJ}^{(l)}

as claimed.
∎

15
Proofs of results from the main text

Proof of Corollary
2.10

Proof.

Infinite divisibility of
\mu^{(i,n)}
simply follows by identifying the Laplace transform of the
\mu^{(i,n)}
to have Lévy-Khintchine characteristics
(\alpha/n,\nu/n)
. Since
\mu
is finite on every localizing set
U_{i}
, the
\mu^{(i,n)}
must as well be almost surely finite on every localizing set. Moreover, since the
\mu^{(i,n)}
are i.i.d., they must satisfy
\mu^{(i,n)}({\bm{\infty}})={\bm{\infty}}
almost surely. Thus, they are random exponent measure almost surely. Identifying each
\mu^{(i,n)}
with a version that is an exponent measure for every realization, we obtain the first claim. The second claim simply follows by observing that each measure in
M_{d}^{0}
is the exponent measure of a random vector with
{\bm{\infty}}
as upper bound of its support, which shows that (
6
) is simply a sum of exponent measures with
{\bm{\infty}}
as common upper bound of the support of the underlying random vectors.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.11

Proof.

To prove that
{\underline{\bm{X}}}
is an exchangeable min-id sequence it is sufficient to prove that its
p
-dimensional margins are min-id, since exchangeability is obvious by construction. To this purpose, let
p\in\mathbb{N}
and choose a
p
-dimensional margin
IJ=(i_{1},j_{1}),\ldots,(i_{p},j_{p})
. Denote by
J
the unique indices in
(j_{k})_{1\leq k\leq p}
. Define the set
I(j):=\{i\mid(i,j)\in IJ\mid\}
to collect the
i
-indices in IJ with common index
j
, i.e. the indices in
I(j)
correspond to all components of the random vector
{\bm{X}}_{j}
that appear in
{\bm{X}}_{IJ}
. The key observation now is that

\displaystyle S_{IJ}({\bm{x}}_{IJ}):=P\left({\bm{X}}_{IJ}>{\bm{x}}_{IJ}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[P\left({\bm{X}}_{IJ}>{\bm{x}}_{IJ}\mid\mu\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{j\in J}\exp\left(-\mu(A_{j})\right)\right],

where for all
j\in J
we denote
A_{j}:=\{{\bm{y}}\in E_{d}\mid y_{i}\leq x_{i,j}\text{ for some }i\in I(j)\}
. Due to the infinite divisibility of
\mu
, we obtain
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{j\in J}\exp\left(-\mu(A_{j})\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}\left(\sum_{j\in J}\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}({\bm{x}})\right)\mu(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}\left(\sum_{j\in J}\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}({\bm{x}})\right)\alpha(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})-\int_{M_{d}^{0}}1-\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}\left(\sum_{j\in J}\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}({\bm{x}})\right)\eta(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)\right).

To obtain that
{\bm{X}_{IJ}}
is min-id it is sufficient to show that
S_{IJ}^{1/n}(\cdot)
is a survival function for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
\mu
is an IDEM, there exist i.i.d. exponent measures
\left(\mu^{(i,n)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq n}
such that
\mu\sim\sum_{{1\leq i\leq n}}\mu^{(i,n)}
, which are themselves IDEMs with Lévy-Khintchine characteristics
(\alpha/n,\nu/n)
by Corollary
2.10
. By repeating the calculations above,
\mu^{(i,n)}
defines the law of an exchangeable sequence with
p
-dimensional marginal survival function
S_{IJ}^{1/n}
. Thus,
S_{IJ}
is the survival function of a min-id random vector. Therefore,
{\underline{\bm{X}}}
is an exchangeable min-id sequence and there must exist an exponent measure
\lambda
on
(E_{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
such that the unconditional distribution of
{\underline{\bm{X}}}
is
\minid(\lambda)
.

It remains to verify that
\lambda
has the claimed decomposition. From the calculations above it follows that for every
{\underline{\bm{x}}}\in[-\infty,\infty)^{\mathbb{N}}
and
A_{\underline{\bm{x}}}:=\{\underline{{\bm{y}}}\in\left(E_{d}\right)^{\mathbb{N}}\mid y_{i,j}\leq x_{i,j}\text{ for some }

{(i,j)\in d\times\mathbb{N}}\}
,
\lambda
has the representation

\displaystyle\lambda(A_{\underline{\bm{x}}})=\int_{E_{d}}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}({\bm{x}})\right)\alpha(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}1-\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{j}}({\bm{x}})\right)\eta(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha(A_{j})+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}1-\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\exp\left(-\eta({A_{j}})\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\alpha}(A_{\underline{\bm{x}}})+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}1-\prod_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\text{min-id}(\eta)\left(\{\bm{y}\in E_{d}\mid y_{i}>x_{i,j}\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq d\}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\alpha}(A_{\underline{\bm{x}}})+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}1-\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\text{min-id}(\eta)\left(\{\underline{\bm{y}}\in\left(E_{d}\right)^{\mathbb{N}}\mid y_{i,j}>x_{i,j}\text{ for all }{(i,j)\in d\times\mathbb{N}}\}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\alpha}(A_{\underline{\bm{x}}})+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\text{min-id}(\eta)\left(\{\underline{\bm{y}}\in\left(E_{d}\right)^{\mathbb{N}}\mid y_{i,j}\leq x_{i,j}\text{ for some }{(i,j)\in d\times\mathbb{N}}\}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\alpha}(A_{\underline{\bm{x}}})+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\text{min-id}(\eta)\left(A_{\underline{\bm{x}}}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta).

where
\lambda_{\alpha}
is defined as the measure
\lambda_{\alpha}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\otimes_{i=1}^{j-1}\delta_{{\bm{\infty}}}\otimes\alpha\otimes_{i=j+1}^{\infty}\delta_{{\bm{\infty}}}
, which is the exponent measure of an i.i.d. sequence of min-id random vectors.
Now, an inclusion-exclusion type argument provides that

\lambda=\lambda_{\alpha}+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\otimes_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\minid(\eta)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

on a
\pi
-stable generator of the Borel
\sigma
-algebra of
E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}
as follows. First, by induction, one can show that for all
IJ=(i_{k},j_{k})_{1\leq k\leq p}\subset d\times\mathbb{N}
and
\bm{a},\bm{b}\in[-\infty,\infty)^{p}
with
\bm{a}<\bm{b}
we have

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\in E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\mid y_{i_{k},j_{k}}\in(a_{k},b_{k}]\ \forall\ 1\leq k\leq p\}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{c_{i}\in\{a_{i},b_{i}\}^{p}}(-1)^{|\{k\in\{1,\ldots,p\}\mid c_{k}=b_{k}\}|}\lambda\left(\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\in E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\mid y_{i_{k},j_{k}}\leq c_{k}\text{ for some }1\leq k\leq n\}\right),

We have already shown that the expression on the right hand side is identical to

\displaystyle\lambda_{\alpha}\left(A_{{\underline{\bm{x}}}(\bm{c})}\right)+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\text{min-id}(\eta)\left(A_{{\underline{\bm{x}}}(\bm{c})}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

where
{\underline{\bm{x}}}(\bm{c}):=\times_{(i,j)\in d\times\mathbb{N}}\left(c_{k}\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)\in IJ\}}-\infty\mathbf{1}_{(i,j)\not\in IJ}\right)
. This uniquely determines
\lambda
on

\bigg\{\{{\underline{\bm{x}}}\in E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\mid x_{i_{k},j_{k}}\in(a_{i},b_{i}]\ \forall\ 1\leq k\leq p\},\bm{a},\bm{b}\in[-\infty,\infty)^{p},\bm{a}<\bm{b},IJ=\left((i_{k},j_{k})\right)_{1\leq k\leq p},p\in\mathbb{N}\bigg\}.

Noticing that by setting
a_{p}
and considering

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\in E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\mid y_{i_{k},j_{k}}\in(a_{k},b_{k}]\ \forall\ 1\leq k\leq p-1,y_{i_{p},j_{p}}=\infty\}\right)=

\displaystyle\lambda\left(\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\in E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\mid y_{i_{k},j_{k}}\in(a_{k},b_{k}]\ \forall\ 1\leq k\leq p-1\}\right)

\displaystyle-\lim_{b_{p}\to\infty}\lambda\left(\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}\in E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\mid y_{i_{k},j_{k}}\in(a_{k},b_{k}]\ \forall\ 1\leq k\leq p\}\right)

we can determine
\lambda
to be equal to
\lambda_{\alpha}+\lambda_{\nu}
on sets of the form

\displaystyle\Big\{\big\{{\underline{\bm{x}}}\in E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\ \big|x_{i_{k},j_{k}}\in(a_{i},b_{i}]\ \forall\ 1\leq k\leq p\big\},\bm{a},\bm{b}\in[-\infty,\infty]^{p},\bm{a}<\bm{b}<{\bm{\infty}}

\displaystyle,IJ=\left((i_{k},j_{k})\right)_{1\leq k\leq p},p\in\mathbb{N}\Big\},

which is a
\pi
-stable generator of the
\sigma
-algebra on
E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}
. It follows that
\lambda
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\lambda=\lambda_{\alpha}+\lambda_{\nu}.

Concerning the uniqueness, we already know that the law of a min-id sequence is uniquely associated to its exponent measure and that the law of an IDEM is uniquely associated to its Lévy characteristics. Therefore, it solely remains to show that the decomposition of
\lambda
into
\lambda_{\alpha}+\lambda_{\nu}
is unique, i.e. that
\lambda_{\alpha_{1}}+\lambda_{\nu_{1}}=\lambda=\lambda_{\alpha_{2}}+\lambda_{\nu_{2}}
implies that
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}
and
\nu_{1}=\nu_{2}
. The uniqueness of
\alpha
simply follows from the fact that
\lambda_{\alpha}
is supported on
\mathcal{W}
with
\lambda_{\nu}(\mathcal{W})=0
, since for every
\eta
with
\eta(E^{\prime}_{d})\not=0
we have
\minid(\eta)({\bm{\infty}})\in[0,1)
which gives

\displaystyle\lambda_{\nu}\left(\mathcal{W}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\otimes_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid(\eta)\left(\{{\underline{\bm{x}}}\in E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\mid{\bm{x}}_{j}={\bm{\infty}}\text{ for all but one }j\}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle=\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\minid(\eta)(E_{d})\prod_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\minid(\eta)({\bm{\infty}})\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)=0.

As
\lambda
is unique,
\lambda_{\alpha_{1}}
and
\lambda_{\alpha_{2}}
must coincide on
\mathcal{W}
, and thus
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}
. Next, consider
\lambda_{\nu}
and recall that the distribution of an IDEM is uniquely determined by its Lévy characteristics. Thus, when
\nu_{1}\not=\nu_{2}
there must exist two IDEMs
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
which are not identical in law but yield the same exchangeable min-id sequence. To this purpose, recall that an IDRM is uniquely determined by its evaluations on sets of the form
({\bm{x}},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}
, where
{\bm{x}}\in[-\infty,\infty)^{d}
. Since
\mu\left(({\bm{x}},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\geq 0
, it is thus sufficient to show that the Laplace transform of
\left(\mu\left(({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)_{1\leq i\leq n}
is uniquely determined by
\lambda
, which then implies that
(\alpha_{1},\nu_{1})
and
(\alpha_{2},\nu_{2})
must coincide. Therefore, for
\left(a_{i}\right)_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
recall that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}-a_{i}\mu\left(({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\lambda\left(\left(\times_{i=1}^{a_{1}}({\bm{x}}_{1},{\bm{\infty}}]\times\ldots\times_{i=1}^{a_{n}}({\bm{x}}_{n},{\bm{\infty}}]\times E_{d\times\mathbb{N}}\right)^{\complement}\right)\right).

Next, recall that the law of a non-negative random vector is uniquely determined by its Laplace transform on
\mathbb{N}^{n}
, see e.g.
[
25
, p. 8]
. Thus, if there are two IDEMs
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
with different Lévy characteristics
(\alpha_{1},\nu_{1})
and
(\alpha_{2},\nu_{2})
there exists
(a_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
and
({\bm{x}}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}\in[-{\bm{\infty}},{\bm{\infty}})^{n}
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\mu_{i}\left(({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\right]\not=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\mu_{2}\left(({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\right].

However, this implies that
\lambda_{\alpha_{1}}+\lambda_{\nu_{1}}\not=\lambda_{\alpha_{2}}+\lambda_{\nu_{2}}
and we have a contradiction. Thus,
\lambda
uniquely corresponds to a tuple of Lévy characteristics
(\alpha,\nu)
and the law of
{\underline{\bm{X}}}
is uniquely determined by
(\alpha,\nu)
.

The converse statement that every
\lambda
of the form
\lambda=\lambda_{\alpha}+\lambda_{\nu}
for some Lévy characteristics
(\alpha,\nu)
is uniquely associated to an IDEM follows similarly.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.2

Proof.

The IDEM property of
\mu
is obvious in all three cases. Therefore, we focus on determining the Lévy characteristics in each case.

(i)

Note that
\mu_{g}
is the image measure of
\mu
under
g
. Thus,
\int_{E_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{g}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})=\int_{E_{d}}f(g({\bm{x}}))\mathrm{d}\mu
, which gives us that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{g}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}f(g({\bm{x}}))\alpha(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})-\int_{M_{d}^{0}}1-\exp\left(-\int f(g({\bm{x}}))\eta(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\alpha_{g}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})-\int_{M_{d}^{0}}1-\exp\left(-\int f({\bm{x}})\eta(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\nu_{g}(\mathrm{d}\eta)\right)

for every non-negative measurable
f:E_{d}\to[0,\infty)
. Thus,
\mu_{g}
has characteristic triplet
(\alpha_{g},\nu_{g})
.

(ii)

We note that
\mu\sim\alpha+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\eta N(\mathrm{d}\eta)
for a PRM
N=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\delta_{\eta_{i}}
on
M_{d}^{0}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mu^{(g)}(A)

\displaystyle=\int_{A}g({\bm{x}})\alpha(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})+\int_{A}g({\bm{x}})\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\eta_{i}\right)(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})=\alpha^{(g)}(A)+\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{A}g({\bm{x}})\eta_{i}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\alpha^{(g)}(A)+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{A}\eta_{i}^{(g)}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})=\alpha^{(g)}(A)+\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\eta^{(g)}(A)N(\mathrm{d}\eta),

where we can interchange the limit in the integrator and the integration since
\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\int_{A}g({\bm{x}})\eta_{i}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})
is a monotone increasing sequence. Note that
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\delta_{\eta_{i}^{(g)}}
is again a PRM with intensity measure
\nu\left(\{\eta\mid\eta^{(g)}\in\cdot\}\right)
, since the map
\eta\mapsto\eta^{(g)}
is measurable.
Thus, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\mu^{(g)}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}g({\bm{x}})f({\bm{x}})\alpha(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\eta^{(g)}\right)(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\alpha^{(g)}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})-\int_{M_{d}^{0}}1-\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\eta(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\nu^{(g)}(\mathrm{d}\eta)\right)

for every non-negative measurable
f:E_{d}\to[0,\infty)
, which proves the claim.

(iii)

Observe that

\displaystyle\mu^{(\beta)}\left(A\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{A}\alpha\left((-{\bm{\infty}},{\bm{x}}]\right)\beta(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})+\int_{A}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\eta_{i}\left((-{\bm{\infty}},{\bm{x}}]\right)\beta(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\alpha^{(\beta)}\left(A\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\eta_{i}^{(\beta)}\left(A\right),

where we can interchange integration and summation since
\eta_{i}\left((-{\bm{\infty}},{\bm{x}}]\right)\geq 0
. Now, similar arguments as in
(ii)
yield the claim.

∎

Proof of Proposition
3.3

Proof.

(i)

First, when
\mu_{0}\left((-\infty,t]\right)<\infty
almost surely for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
then

\displaystyle P\left(\mu_{i}\left((-\infty,t]\right)<\infty\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[P\left(\mu_{i}\left((-\infty,t]\right)<\infty\mid\mu_{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[P\left(\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}a\mathrm{d}N^{(i,\mu_{0})}(\mathrm{d}a,\mathrm{d}b)<\infty\mid\mu_{0}\right)\right]=1

since (
9
) is almost surely satisfied by
\mu_{0}
and where we used the notation that
N^{(i,\mu_{0})}
denotes a PRM on
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
with intensity measure
\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\mu_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)
. Thus,
\mu_{i}\left((-\infty,t]\right)<\infty
almost surely for all
1\leq i\leq d
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

To prove the remaining claims recall that the sum of independent CRMs is again a CRM. Further, since
\mu_{0}
is an IDRM, we can find i.i.d. random measures
\left(\mu_{0}^{(j,n)}\right)_{1\leq j\leq n}
such that
\mu_{0}\sim\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu_{0}^{(j,n)}
. Now, conditionally on
\left(\mu_{0}^{(j,n)}\right)_{1\leq j\leq n}
, for every
1\leq j\leq n
we can construct independent vectors of CRMs
\left(\mu_{i}^{(j,n)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq d}
where the entries
\mu_{i}^{(j,n)}
are independent CRMs with Lévy characteristics
(\alpha_{i}/n,\nu_{i}^{(j,n)})
, where
\nu_{i}^{(j,n)}
is the image of
(a,b)\mapsto a\delta_{b}
under
\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}^{(j,n)}
. Since the
\mu_{0}^{(j,n)}
are i.i.d. we get that
\left(\left(\mu_{i}^{(j,n)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq d}\right)_{1\leq j\leq n}
is a collection of
n
i.i.d. vectors of random measures. Moreover, conditionally on
\left(\mu_{0}^{(j,n)}\right)_{1\leq j\leq n}
,
\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mu_{i}^{(j,n)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq d}
is distributed as a vector of independent CRMs with respective base measures
\alpha_{i}
and Lévy measures given as the image measure of
(a,b)\mapsto a\delta_{b}
under
\rho_{i}\mathrm{d}a\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mu_{0}^{(j,n)}(\mathrm{d}b)
. Thus, since
\mu_{0}\sim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mu_{0}^{(j,n)}
, we have
\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mu_{i}^{(j,n)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq d}\sim\left(\mu_{i}\right)_{1\leq i\leq d}
.

For brevity, we omit the details and refer to mimicking the proof of
(ii)
to obtain the claimed Lévy characteristics of
\mu_{i}
.

It remains to prove that when
\mu_{0}
is a CRM then
\left(\mu_{i}\right)_{1\leq i\leq d}
are also CRMs. Obviously,
\nu_{1,i}
is the Lévy measure of a CRM. Further,
\nu_{0}
is concentrated on measures of the form
\underline{A}:=\{\eta\mid\eta=a\delta_{b}+\infty\delta_{\bm{\infty}}\text{ for some }(a,b)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\}
.
In this case,
\crm_{\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\eta(\mathrm{d}b)}
is of the form
\crm_{\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a(a_{0}\delta_{b_{0}})(\mathrm{d}b)}
and thus has at most one finite atom. Therefore,
\nu_{2,i}
also concentrates on measures of the form
\underline{A}
and the sum
\nu_{1,i}+\nu_{2,i}
is still a measure concentrating on
\underline{A}
. Along the lines of the proof of
[
23
, Theorem 3.19]
it is now easy to deduce that every Lévy measure which concentrates on
\underline{A}
is the Lévy measure of a CRM. Thus, the claim is proven.

(ii)

First, it is obvious to see that
\mu_{\mu_{1}^{(\kappa_{1})},\ldots,\mu^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
as defined in (
10
) is an exponent measure whenever
\mu_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}\left((-\infty,t]\right)<\infty
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Thus, it remains to show its infinite divisibility and for this purpose it is sufficient to derive the Lévy characteristics of
\mu_{\mu_{1}^{(\kappa_{1})},\ldots,\mu^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
.

First, observe that
\mu_{i}=\alpha_{i}+\tilde{\mu}_{i}
for all
0\leq i\leq d
, where
\tilde{\mu}_{0}
is an IDRM with Lévy characteristics
(0,\nu_{0})
and
\tilde{\mu}_{i}
is, conditionally on
\mu_{0}
, a CRM without base measure and Lévy measure as the image measure of
(a,b)\mapsto a\delta_{b}
under
\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\mu_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)
. Note that for every non-negative measurable
f
on
E^{\prime}_{d}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{E_{d}^{\prime}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{\mu_{1}^{(\kappa_{1})},\ldots,\mu^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\mu_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\alpha^{(\kappa_{i})}_{i}(\mathrm{d}s)+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,y)\tilde{\mu}^{(\kappa_{i})}_{i}(\mathrm{d}s)

\displaystyle=\int_{E^{\prime}_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{\alpha_{1}^{(\kappa_{1})},\ldots,\alpha^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})+\int_{E^{\prime}_{d}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{\tilde{\mu}_{1}^{(\kappa_{1})},\ldots,\tilde{\mu}^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})

where
{\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s)
has all entries equal to
\infty
expect for the
i
-th entry equal to
s
. Let
\left(\zeta_{i}\right)_{1\leq i\leq d}
denote independent univariate CRMs without base measure and Lévy measure as the image measure of
(a,b)\mapsto a\delta_{b}
under
\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\alpha_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)
. Define

\displaystyle T_{1}:=

\displaystyle-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}^{\prime}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{\zeta^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\zeta^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\zeta_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}(\mathrm{d}s)\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\right)\zeta_{i}(\mathrm{d}y)\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}1-\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))(a\delta_{b})^{(\kappa_{i})}(\mathrm{d}s)\right)\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\alpha_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}1-\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}^{\prime}}f({\bm{x}})\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{i-1}\delta_{\infty}\times(a\delta_{b})^{(\kappa_{i})}\otimes_{j=i+1}^{d}\right)(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\alpha_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)

and

\displaystyle T_{2}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\alpha_{i}(\mathrm{d}y)=\int_{E_{d}^{\prime}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{\alpha_{1}^{(\kappa_{1})},\ldots,\alpha^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}).

We obtain

\displaystyle-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}^{\prime}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{\mu^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\mu_{i}(\mathrm{d}y)\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\right)\mu_{i}(\mathrm{d}y)\right)\ \bigg|\ \alpha_{0},\tilde{\mu}_{0}\right]\right]\right)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\right)\tilde{\mu}_{i}(\mathrm{d}y)-T_{2}\right)\ \bigg|\ \alpha_{0},\tilde{\mu}_{0}\right]\right]\right)

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1-e^{-\int_{\mathbb{R}}af({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,b)\mathrm{d}s}\right)\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle+T_{1}+T_{2}

\displaystyle=-\log\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1-e^{-\int_{\mathbb{R}}af({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,b)\mathrm{d}s}\right)\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\right)\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)\right)\right]\right)

\displaystyle+T_{1}+T_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{M^{0}_{d}}\left(1-\exp\left(-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1-e^{-\int_{\mathbb{R}}af({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,b)\mathrm{d}s}\right)\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\right)\eta(\mathrm{d}b)\right)\right)\nu_{0}(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle+T_{1}+T_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{M^{0}_{d}}\Bigg(1-\exp\Bigg(-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(1-e^{-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\right)(a\delta_{b})(\mathrm{d}y)}\right)\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\eta(\mathrm{d}b)\Bigg)\Bigg)\nu_{0}(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle+T_{1}+T_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\star}{=}\int_{M^{0}_{d}}\left(1-\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)}(s))\kappa_{i}(s,b)\mathrm{d}s\right)\eta_{i}(\mathrm{d}b)}\right]\right)\nu_{0}(\mathrm{d}\eta)+T_{1}+T_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{M^{0}_{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[1-\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}^{\prime}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{\eta^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\eta^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\right]\nu_{0}(\mathrm{d}\eta)+T_{1}+T_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{M^{0}_{d}}\int_{\times_{i=1}^{d}M^{0}_{d}}1-\exp\left(-\int_{E_{d}^{\prime}}f({\bm{x}})\mu_{\eta^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\eta^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})\right)\otimes_{i=1}^{d}\crm_{\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\eta(\mathrm{d}b)}(\mathrm{d}\eta_{i})\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle+T_{1}+T_{2}

where in
\star

\left(\eta_{i}\right)_{1\leq i\leq d}
denote independent CRMs without base measure and intensity
\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\eta(\mathrm{d}b)
and
\mu_{\eta^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\eta^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
denotes an exponent measure constructed as in (
10
). Thus, combining the above we have that
\mu_{\mu^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
has base measure
\mu_{\alpha^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\alpha^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
and Lévy measure
\nu=\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}
, where
\nu_{1}
is given by

\nu_{1}(\eta\in A)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\otimes_{j=1}^{i-1}\delta_{\infty}\times(a\delta_{b})^{(\kappa_{i})}\otimes_{j=i+1}^{d}\delta_{\infty}\in A\}}\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\alpha_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)

and
\nu_{2}
is defined as

\nu_{2}(\eta\in A)=\int_{M^{0}_{d}}\int_{\times_{i=1}^{d}M^{0}_{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\big\{\mu_{\eta_{1}^{(\kappa_{1})},\ldots,\eta_{d}^{(\kappa_{d})}}\in A\big\}}\otimes_{i=1}^{d}\crm_{\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\eta(\mathrm{d}b)}(\mathrm{d}\eta_{i})\nu_{0}(\mathrm{d}\eta)

proving the claims.

∎

Proof of Theorem
4.2

Proof.

Note that every min-id sequence
{\underline{\bm{X}}}
can be viewed as a continuous min-id process with index set
d\times\mathbb{N}
. Condition
2
implies that
{\underline{\bm{X}}}
has continuous marginal distributions and therefore
[
12
, Theorem 3.3]
can be applied to every
{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n}+k
, as their results are only formulated for compact index sets. A translation of their formulas for max-id processes to min-id sequences yields the claimed representation.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.3

Proof.

From (
12
) we can deduce that for every
IJ\subset d\times n
the probability kernel
K_{IJ}({\bm{x}}_{IJ},\cdot)

\lambda^{(IJ)}
-almost surely defines the law of a sequence of random vectors
{\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(IJ)}
such that
\left({\bm{Z}}^{(IJ)}_{n+j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is exchangeable, since otherwise it could be proven that
\lambda
is non-exchangeable on a
\lambda
non-nullset, which would imply that
{\underline{\bm{X}}}
is not exchangeable. The details are left to the reader. Next, by (
14
) and conditionally on
\tilde{\Theta}
, we observe that the law of the sequences
{\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(n,l)}
corresponds to the distribution the sequence
{\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})}\sim K_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},\cdot)
conditionally on
{\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}
. Since
\left({\bm{Z}}_{n+j}^{(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is exchangeable, one can deduce that
{\underline{\bm{Z}}}^{(n,l)}
must be exchangeable as well and the claim is proven.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.4

Proof.

Everything except for the independence of
\overline{\mu}_{n}
and
S_{n}
follows from the discussion above the theorem and Lemma
4.3
.
[
12
, Theorem 3.3 3.]
provides that
\underline{\bm{Y}}^{(n)}
and
\left(\tilde{\Theta},\left(\underline{\bm{Z}}^{(n,l)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq L}\right)
are independent conditionally on
{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n}
. Since
\overline{\mu}_{n}
and
S^{(n,l)}
can be recovered from
\underline{\bm{Y}}^{(n)}
and
\underline{\bm{Z}}^{(n,l)}
, respectively, we obtain their independence.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.2

Proof.

By definition,
K_{IJ}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ},\cdot\right)
is a probability measure on
(-\infty,\infty]^{md-|IJ|}
for every
{\bm{x}}_{IJ}\in E_{|IJ|}^{\prime}
, since
f^{(IJ)}
and
g^{(IJ)}
are the appropriate marginalization of
f^{(d\times m)}
and
g^{(d\times m)}
. It remains to show that for every
\mathsf{G}:E_{|IJ|}\times E_{md-|IJ|}\to[0,\infty)
which vanishes on
\{{\bm{\infty}}\}\times E_{md-|IJ|}
(
12
) holds. We have

\displaystyle\int_{E^{\prime}_{|IJ|}}\int_{E_{md-|IJ|}}\mathsf{G}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ},{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m\setminus IJ}\right)K_{IJ}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ},\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m\setminus IJ}\right)\lambda^{(IJ)}\left(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{IJ}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{E^{\prime}_{|IJ|}}\int_{E_{md-|IJ|}}\mathsf{G}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ},{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m\setminus IJ}\right)\frac{f^{(d\times m)}\left({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m}\right)+g^{(d\times m)}\left({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m}\right)}{f_{IJ}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ}\right)+g^{(IJ)}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ}\right)}\left(\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{md-|IJ|}\right)(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m\setminus IJ})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \left(f^{(IJ)}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ}\right)+g^{(IJ)}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ}\right)\right)\left(\Lambda_{|IJ|}^{(\infty)}\right)(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{IJ})

\displaystyle=\int_{E_{md}}\mathsf{G}\left({\bm{x}}_{IJ},{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m\setminus IJ}\right)\lambda^{(d\times m)}(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m}),

since
f^{(d\times m)}(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m})+g^{(d\times m)}(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m})\left(\otimes_{i=1}^{md}\Lambda^{(\infty)}\right)(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m})=\lambda^{(d\times m)}(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m})
, using that
\mathsf{G}({\bm{\infty}},\cdot)=0
. Thus (
12
) holds and
K_{IJ}
is the regular conditional probability of
\lambda^{(d\times m)}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.3

Proof.

To ease the notation let
m=n+k
and set
\tilde{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m}:=\left({\bm{X}}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\{j\leq d\}}+{\bm{x}}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\{n<j\leq m\}}\right)_{1\leq j\leq m}
and
\hat{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m}:=\left({\bm{X}}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\{j\leq d\}}+{\bm{\infty}}\mathbf{1}_{\{n<j\leq m\}}\right)_{1\leq j\leq m}
. We use Lemma
5.2
, Fubini, the representation of
f^{(IJ)}
in (
20
) and Theorem
4.2
to obtain

\displaystyle P\left(\bm{Z}^{(n,l)}_{i}>{\bm{x}}_{i}\text{ for all }n<i\leq m\mid\tilde{\Theta},{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\left({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\in E_{md-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\mid{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>\tilde{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}\right)}{K_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\left({\bm{X}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}},\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\in E_{nd-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\mid{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}\right)}

\displaystyle=C({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{-1}\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>\tilde{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}}\left(f^{(d\times m)}+g^{(d\times m)}\right)\left((\tilde{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m},{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m})(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\right)\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{md-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right)

\displaystyle=C({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{-1}\Bigg(\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>\tilde{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}f_{\eta}\left((\tilde{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m},{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m})(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\right)\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{md-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle+\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>\tilde{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}}g^{(d\times m)}\left((\tilde{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m},{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m})(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\right)\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{md-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{\star}{=}C({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{-1}\Bigg(\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\int_{\{{\bm{y}}_{n+j}>{\bm{x}}_{j}\forall 1\leq j\leq k\}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}f_{\eta}({\bm{y}}_{n+i})\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\eta}\left(({\bm{X}}_{i},{\bm{y}}_{i})(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \Lambda^{(\infty)}_{nd-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right)\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{kd}\left(\mathrm{d}\left({\bm{y}}_{n+j}\right)_{1\leq j\leq k}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle+\int_{\{{\bm{y}}_{n+j}={\bm{\infty}}\forall 1\leq j\leq k,{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}}g^{(d\times m)}\left((\hat{{\underline{\bm{X}}}}_{m},{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m})(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\right)\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{md-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{m\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{\star}{=}C({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l})^{-1}\Bigg(\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\underset{1\leq j\leq k}{\otimes}\minid(\eta)\left(\big\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{k}\mid{\bm{y}}_{j}>{\bm{x}}_{j}\ \forall\ 1\leq j\leq k\big\}\right)h(\eta,{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \Lambda^{(\infty)}_{nd-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right)\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{kd}\left(\mathrm{d}\left({\bm{y}}_{n+j}\right)_{1\leq j\leq k}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle+\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}}g^{(d\times n)}\left(({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n})(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\right)\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{nd-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right)\Bigg),

where

\displaystyle C({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l}):=

\displaystyle\int_{M_{d}^{0}}h(\eta,{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)

\displaystyle+\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}}g^{(d\times n)}\left(({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n})(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\right)\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{nd-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right)

and

h(\eta,{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l}):=\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f_{\eta}\left(({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n})(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\right)\left(\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{nd-|\tilde{\Theta}_{l}|}\right)\left(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\Theta}_{l}}\right).

Note that in
\star
we have repeatedly used that
{\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n}
is real-valued and that
g^{(d\times m)}
is concentrated on
\mathcal{W}
. Moreover,
C({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l})
is real-valued since
C({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta}_{l})\leq f^{(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})}({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}})+g^{(\tilde{\Theta}_{l})}({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{\tilde{\Theta}_{l}})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.4

Proof.

The distribution of the conditional hitting scenario can be characterized analytically. Recalling (
30
), we define the family of measures
\left(\beta_{\theta}\right)_{\theta\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}
via
\beta_{\theta}(A):=P\left({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n}\in A,\Theta=\theta\right)
. Trivially, each
\beta_{\theta}
is absolutely continuous w.r.t.
\beta:=\sum_{\theta\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\beta_{\theta}
with a density
d\beta_{\theta}/d\beta
on
\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
. Now,
[
12
, Theorem 3.2.1]
provides that

\tau({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n},\tilde{\Theta})=\frac{d\beta_{\tilde{\Theta}}}{d\beta}({\underline{\bm{X}}}_{n}).

and by (
30
)
\beta_{\theta}(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n})
is given by

\exp\left(-\lambda^{(d\times n)}\left(({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}K_{\theta_{l}}\left({\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}},\big\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\theta_{l}}\mid{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\theta_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n\setminus\theta_{l}}\big\}\right)\otimes_{l=1}^{L}\lambda^{(\theta_{l})}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}}).

Noting that

\otimes_{l=1}^{L(\Theta)}\lambda^{(\Theta_{l})}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}_{\Theta_{l}})=\left(\prod_{l=1}^{L(\Theta)}f^{(\Theta_{l})}({\bm{x}}_{\Theta_{l}})+g^{(\Theta_{l})}({\bm{x}}_{\Theta_{l}})\right)\left(\Lambda_{nd}^{(\infty)}\right)(\mathrm{d}{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n})

we get that
\beta
and
\beta_{\theta}
both have a density w.r.t.
\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{nd}
. Thus,
\frac{d\beta_{\theta}}{d\beta}
is given by

\displaystyle\frac{\exp\left(-\lambda^{(d\times n)}\left(({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}K_{\theta_{l}}\left({\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}},\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\theta_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n\setminus\theta_{l}}\}\right)\prod_{l=1}^{L(\theta)}\left(f^{(\theta_{l})}+g^{(\theta_{l})}\right)({\bm{x}}_{\theta_{l}})}{\sum_{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\exp\left(-\lambda^{(d\times n)}\left(({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n},{\bm{\infty}}]^{\complement}\right)\right)\prod_{l=1}^{L(\tilde{\theta})}K_{\tilde{\theta}_{l}}\left({\bm{x}}_{\tilde{\theta}_{l}},\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\theta}_{l}}\}\right)\prod_{l=1}^{L(\tilde{\theta})}\left(f^{(\tilde{\theta}_{l})}+g^{(\tilde{\theta}_{l})}\right)({\bm{x}}_{\tilde{\theta}_{l}})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{L(\theta)}\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\theta_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n\setminus\theta_{l}}\}}\left(f^{(d\times n)}+g^{(d\times n)}\right)\left(({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n},\underline{{\bm{y}}}_{n})(\tilde{\theta}_{l})\right)\left(\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{nd-|\theta_{l}|}\right)\mathrm{d}\left({\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\theta_{l}}\right)}{\sum_{\tilde{\theta}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{L(\tilde{\theta})}\int_{\{{\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\theta}_{l}}>{\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\theta}_{l}}\}}\left(f^{(d\times n)}+g^{(d\times n)}\right)\left(({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n},\underline{{\bm{y}}}_{n})(\tilde{\theta}_{l})\right)\left(\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{nd-|\tilde{\theta}_{l}|}\right)\mathrm{d}\left({\underline{\bm{y}}}_{n\setminus\tilde{\theta}_{l}}\right)}

where we used the representation of
K_{IJ}
from Lemma
5.2
and
({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{n},\underline{{\bm{y}}}_{n})(\theta_{l})=\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)\in\theta_{l}\}}x_{i,j}+y_{i,j}\mathbf{1}_{\{(i,j)\not\in\theta_{l}\}}\big)_{(i,j)\in d\times n}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.1

Proof.

First we need to show that
\mu_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}([0,t))<\infty
for all
t>0
.
Note that by Condition
4
we have that for every
t>0
we can find a
C>0
such that
\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\kappa_{i}(s,b)\mu_{i}(\mathrm{d}b)\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{C}\kappa_{i}(s,b)\mu_{i}(\mathrm{d}b)\mathrm{d}s
. Thus, since
\kappa_{i}
is also bounded by Condition
4
,
\mu_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}([0,t))<\infty
for all
t>0
if and only if
\mu_{i}\left([0,C)\right)<\infty
for all
C>0
. It is easy to see that
\mu_{i}\left([0,C)\right)<\infty
if
\mu^{(\kappa_{0})}_{0}\left([0,C)\right)<\infty
. By the same arguments as above,
\mu^{(\kappa_{0})}_{0}\left([0,C)\right)<\infty
if and only if
\int_{0}^{\bar{C}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\min\{a_{0},1\}l(a_{0},b_{0})\mathrm{d}a_{0}\mathrm{d}b_{0}<\infty
for all
\bar{C}>0
, which is satisfied by Condition
4
.

Next, we show that
\mu_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}([0,t))>0
for all
t>0
. To see that
\mu_{0}([0,t))>0
almost surely notice that
\int_{0}^{\infty}l(a_{0},b_{0})\mathrm{d}a_{0}=\infty
for every
b_{0}>0
together with
l(a_{0},b_{0})>0
implies that
\mu_{0}
has infinitely many non-zero atoms which are dense in
[0,t)
, thus
\mu_{0}([0,t))>0
almost surely. This also implies that
\mu_{0}^{(\kappa_{0})}([0,t))>0
since for every non-zero atom
b
of
\mu_{0}
we use Condition
4
to find a
\bar{b}>b
such that
\kappa_{0}(\tilde{s},b)>0
for all
\tilde{s}\in(b,\bar{b})
, which implies
\int_{\{b\}}\int_{b}^{\bar{b}}\kappa_{0}(\tilde{s},\tilde{b})\mathrm{d}\tilde{s}\mu_{0}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{b})=\mu_{0}(\{b\})\int_{b}^{\bar{b}}\kappa_{0}(\tilde{s},b)\mathrm{d}\tilde{s}>0
, implying
\mu_{0}^{(\kappa_{0})}((b,\bar{b}))>0
. Therefore,
\mu_{i}([0,t))>0
almost surely, since
\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a=\infty
and, conditionally on
\mu_{0}
, the PRM representation of
\mu_{i}
has infinitely many non-zero atoms in
[0,t)]
. Similarly as before, we can now deduce that
\mu_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}([0,t))>0
almost surely.

Let us show that
\mu_{\mu^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
is an IDEM. For
\int_{0}^{\infty}\kappa_{i}(s,b)\mathrm{d}s=\infty
we now obviously have
\lim_{t\to\infty}\mu_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}([0,t))=\infty
and
\mu_{\mu^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
is an IDEM. If
\mu_{0}([0,\infty))=\infty
we have
\mu_{0}^{(\kappa_{0})}([0,\infty))=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\kappa_{0}(s,b)\mathrm{d}s\mu_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)\geq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{b}^{b+\delta}C\mathrm{d}s\mu_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)=\infty
. Therefore,
\mu_{i}([0,\infty))=\infty
almost surely and the analogous argument yields
\mu_{i}^{(\kappa_{i})}([0,\infty))=\infty
. Thus,
\mu_{\mu^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
is an IDEM and Proposition
3.2
in combination with Proposition
3.3

(ii)
provides that the Lévy measure has the claimed form.

It remains to verify Condition
3
. Since for each
1\leq i\leq d

\mu_{i}^{(\kappa)}([0,\infty))=\infty
we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(-\mu^{(\kappa_{i})}_{i}([0,t])\right)\right]=\exp\left(-\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\minid(\eta)\left(\{{\bm{x}}\in E_{d}\mid x_{i}\leq t\}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)\right)\to 0
for
t\to\infty
, i.e.
\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\minid(\eta)\left(\{{\bm{x}}\in E_{d}\mid x_{i}\leq t\}\right)\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)=\infty
. Furthermore, from the representation of
\nu
it is easy to see that
\nu
-almost-every
\eta
has a density w.r.t.
\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{d}
of the claimed form, which shows that Condition
3
is satisfied.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.2

Proof.

First, note that the density of
\mu_{\eta^{(\kappa_{1})}_{1},\ldots,\eta^{(\kappa_{d})}_{d}}
w.r.t.
\Lambda^{(\infty)}_{d}
is
\prod_{i=1}^{d}e_{\eta_{i},\kappa_{i}}(x_{i})
. We use the general formula for the density
f^{(d\times m)}
defined in (
19
) to get

\displaystyle f^{(d\times m)}({\underline{\bm{x}}}_{m})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{1}_{\{x_{k,j}=\infty\forall(k,j)\in d\times m,k\not=i\}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m}e_{a\delta_{b},\kappa_{i}}(x_{i,j})\right)\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\alpha^{(\kappa_{0})}_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\times_{i=1}^{d}M^{0}_{d}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\prod_{i=1}^{d}e_{\eta_{i},\kappa_{i}}(x_{i,j})\otimes_{i=1}^{d}\crm_{\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a(a_{0}\delta_{b_{0}})^{(\kappa_{0})}(\mathrm{d}b)}(\mathrm{d}\eta_{i})l(a_{0},b_{0})\mathrm{d}a_{0}\mathrm{d}b_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbf{1}_{\{x_{k,j}=\infty\forall(k,j)\in d\times m,k\not=i\}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\prod_{j=1}^{m}e_{a\delta_{b},\kappa_{i}}(x_{i,j})\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a\alpha^{(\kappa_{0})}_{0}(\mathrm{d}b)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{i}\sim\crm_{\rho_{i}(a)\mathrm{d}a(a_{0}\delta_{b_{0}})^{(\kappa_{0})}(\mathrm{d}b)}}\bigg[\prod_{j=1}^{m}e_{\eta_{i},\kappa_{i}}(x_{i,j})\bigg]l(a_{0},b_{0})\mathrm{d}a_{0}\mathrm{d}b_{0}

Using (
20
), the claimed result immediately follows.
∎

16
Technical results on random measures and their support

Given the sequence of measurable sets
(U_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in (
5
) we may construct a localizing ring by
\hat{S}:=\cup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{B}\left(E^{\prime}_{d}\right)\cap S_{n}
, where
S_{n}:=\cup_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}
. According to
[
23
, Chapter 2]
a random measure on (the standard Borel space)
E^{\prime}_{d}
is a random element in the space

\{\mu\mid\mu\text{ is measure on $E^{\prime}_{d}$ which is finite on every set in }\hat{S}\},

which is equipped with the Borel
\sigma
-algebra generated by all evaluations of measures on Borel sets of
E^{\prime}_{d}
. We show that this definition coincides with our definition of a random measure from Definition
2.7

Proposition 16.1
.

\mu
is a random measure on
E^{\prime}_{d}
with localizing ring
\hat{S}
in the sense of
[
23
, Chapter 2]
if and only if it can be extended to a random measure on
E_{d}
with localizing sets
(U_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
in the sense of Definition
2.7
.

Proof.

Note that the
\sigma
-algebra that defines measurability of a random measure on
E^{\prime}_{d}
is identical in both definitions.
Thus, it remains to show that the finiteness of
\mu
on the localizing sets
(U_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is equivalent to finiteness of
\mu
on the localizing ring of
E^{\prime}_{d}
.

Obviously, if
\mu
is a random measure on
E^{\prime}_{d}
with localizing ring
\hat{S}
we have
\mu\left(U_{i}\right)<\infty
, since each
U_{i}
is measurable and thus an element of the localizing ring. Therefore, a random measure in the sense of
[
23
, Chapter 2]
is a random measure on
E^{\prime}_{d}
according to Definition
2.7
.

For the converse, we solely need to show that Definition
2.7
implies that
\mu
is finite on every set
A\in\hat{S}
. By the definition of
\hat{S}
there exists a Borel set
B
on
E^{\prime}_{d}
and an
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
A=B\cap\cup_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}
. Therefore
\mu(A)<\infty
if
\mu(U_{i})<\infty
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
, which proves the claim.

Finally, it remains to observe that setting
\mu({\bm{\infty}})=\infty
is a measurable extension of the random measure
\mu
on
E^{\prime}_{d}
to a random measure on
E_{d}
. To see this, note that
\{\omega\in\Omega\mid\mu(C)\in D\}=\{\omega\in\Omega\mid\mu(C\setminus\{{\bm{\infty}}\})\in D\}
for every measurable
C\subset E^{\prime}_{d}
and measurable
D\subset[0,\infty]
. Moreover,
\{\omega\in\Omega\mid\mu(C)\in D\}=\emptyset\mathbf{1}_{\{\infty\not\in D\}}+\Omega\mathbf{1}_{\{\infty\in D\}}
for every measurable
C\subset E_{d}
containing
{\bm{\infty}}
and every measurable
D\subset[0,\infty]
. Thus, its now easy to see that the evaluation functional
C\mapsto\mu(C)
is measurable for every measurable
C\subset E_{d}
, which implies measurability of
\mu
as a random measure on
E_{d}
.
∎

Further, we show that if an IDEM
\mu
has deterministic support, we may transfer this property to its corresponding Lévy characteristics.

Lemma 16.2
.

Assume that an IDEM
\mu
on
E_{d}
with Lévy characteristic
(\alpha,\nu)
has support that is almost surely contained in a measurable set
S\subseteq E_{d}
. Then,
\alpha\left(S^{\complement}\right)=0
and
\nu
is concentrated on
\big\{\eta\mid\eta\left(S^{\complement}\right)=0\big\}
.

Proof.

We prove the claim by contradiction. To this purpose, define
A:=\big\{\eta\mid\eta\left(S^{\complement}\right)>0\big\}
and assume
\mu\left(S^{\complement}\right)=0
almost surely but
\nu(A)>0
. Note that
A
is measurable since
S
is measurable. Moreover, if
\nu(A)>0
there exists
a>0
s.t.
A_{a}:=\big\{\eta\mid\eta\left(S^{\complement}\right)>a\big\}
satisfies
\nu\left(A_{a}\right)>0
. Therefore, since
\mu\sim\alpha+\int_{M^{0}_{d}}\eta N(\mathrm{d}\eta)=\alpha+\int_{M^{0}_{d}\cap A}\eta N(\mathrm{d}\eta)+\int_{M^{0}_{d}\cap A^{\complement}}\eta N(\mathrm{d}\eta)
, we get
0=\mu\left(S^{\complement}\right)=\alpha\left(S^{\complement}\right)+\int_{M^{0}_{d}\cap A}\eta\left(S^{\complement}\right)N(\mathrm{d}\eta)>aN\left(A_{a}\right)
almost surely. However, since
\nu(A_{a})>0
we have
P(N(A_{a})>0)=1-\exp(-\nu(A_{a}))>0
which is a contradiction.
∎

This also has implications for the support of the posterior of an IDEM.

Corollary 16.3
.

If
\mu
is an IDEM which is concentrated on some set
S\subset E_{d}
, then
\overline{\mu}_{n}
and
\left(\overline{\mu}_{n}^{(l)}\right)_{1\leq i\leq L(\tilde{\Theta})}
from Theorem
5.5
are concentrated on
S
as well. Therefore, the posterior of an IDEM
\mu
inherits the support of the prior.

For example, when
\minid(\mu)
concentrates on survival functions of random vectors with independent components, i.e.
\mu
concentrates on
\cup_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, the posterior of
\overline{\mu}_{n}
and
\left(\overline{\mu}^{(l)}\right)_{1\leq l\leq L(\tilde{\Theta})}
concentrate on
\cup_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{E}_{i}
as well.

Lemma 16.4
.

The Lévy measure of an IDEM on
E_{d}
is
\sigma
-finite.

Proof.

Let
A_{i,\epsilon}:=\{\eta\in M^{0}_{d}\mid\eta(U_{i})\geq\epsilon\}
and define the countable collection of sets
(A_{i,1/j})_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}
. Moreover, for every
\eta\in M^{0}_{d}
we have
\eta(U_{i})>0
for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
because otherwise
\eta(E_{d}^{\prime})=0
. Thus,
M_{d}^{0}=\cup_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}A_{i,1/j}
and it remains to show
\nu\left(A_{i,1/j}\right)<\infty
for all
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
. We know that
\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\min\{\eta(U_{i});1\}\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)<\infty
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
by Theorem
2.9
and therefore
\nu(A_{i,1/j})=\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\eta(U_{i})\geq 1/j\}}\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)\leq j\int_{M_{d}^{0}}\min\{\eta(U_{i});1\}\nu(\mathrm{d}\eta)<\infty
, which proves the claim.
∎
